a:T675,The core of the work is the OctoLong data pipeline, which combines three components: an Abstract Syntax Tree (AST) parser, a Language Server Protocol (LSP) backend, and a package manager, to recursively retrieve cross-repository code dependencies. The pipeline works as follows: 1) Curate high-quality Python repositories (≥15 GitHub stars, ≥5 contributors); 2) Use LSP's type- and scope-aware analysis, paired with package manager-resolved dependencies, to recursively fetch implementations of imported classes and functions across repository boundaries; 3) Concatenate these dependencies in call order to form high-density long code contexts up to millions of tokens in length. Two corpora are built from this pipeline: the ~6.2B-token cross-repository long-context code dataset, mixed with other long-text data to form the ~50B-token OctoLong-LCFT corpus for mid-training long-context continued pretraining; and the ~10B-token OctoLong-SFT mix (open instruction data + agent data) for supervised fine-tuning. The training pipeline starts from Qwen3 dense base models (600M to 14B parameters), first performs LCFT to extend the context window to 128K (4x the native length), then runs SFT to produce the OctoLong-Instruct suite. The key difference from prior work: existing cross-document long-context construction relies on weak correlation signals with low context coupling, while single-repository code serialization is limited by repository boundaries. OctoLong achieves precise cross-repository dependency retrieval via LSP, producing tightly coupled multi-hop dependency contexts that scale infinitely with the open-source package ecosystem.b:T41a,Reading priority for deep dive: 1) Section 3 (OctoLong data pipeline): focus on how LSP, AST, and the package manager work together, and the implementation logic of recursive cross-repository dependency retrieval (see Figure 2 for the pipeline flowchart) — this is the core innovation of the paper. 2) Section 4 (training pipeline): focus on the mid-training LCFT recipe details, including data mixture ratio, training steps, and context window configuration, as well as the composition of the SFT dataset. 3) Section 5 (experiments and ablations): focus on two key comparisons — the long-context performance gap between cross-repository code and single-repository code / general long text, and the retention of short-context capabilities after long-context training. Open directions for further exploration: quantitative metrics for long-context dependency density, scaling laws for mid-training context extension, and whether cross-document dependency mining methods can be transferred to non-code domains such as academic papers and web pages.c:T51a,The baseline Sinkhorn matrix optimizer repeatedly normalizes the rows and columns of a matrix gradient and applies a scalar update scale. It is attractive because it removes moment state for dense two-dimensional weights. SAGE-like hybrids typically keep lightweight state for vocabulary tensors and use Sinkhorn for dense matrices. The paper shows that this dense-model role assignment breaks for MoE experts. For expert e, the gradient G_{e,t} is drawn from a changing conditional distribution p(x | e is selected by top-k routing), not from the full minibatch distribution. Since Sinkhorn normalization is nonlinear, Sinkhorn(E[G_t]) and E[Sinkhorn(G_t)] can behave differently. MESH addresses this by maintaining a hidden first-moment signal H_t for expert gradients and applying Sinkhorn to H_t rather than to G_t. Non-expert matrices and vocabulary tensors keep the SAGE/Sinkhorn hybrid treatment. The expert momentum is maintained through the gradient-buffer lifecycle instead of being stored as an explicit optimizer-state tensor. MESH-B adds a coarse block/neuron inverse-RMS multiplier, giving a limited form of adaptive scaling. The paper’s own interpretation is conservative: block-level adaptivity can help, but temporal smoothing before Sinkhorn normalization is the main causal ingredient.d:T43b,Proposes MALT (Muon Augmented by Lightweight Two-sided preconditioning) and its adaptive variant MALTER: 1) Derives a preconditioned Muon formulation based on local quadratic approximation of the loss, using a positive-definite curvature surrogate to construct a transformed space with isotropic quadratic terms, then orthogonalizes preconditioned momentum and maps the direction back to the original parameter space to address both curvature and gradient anisotropy simultaneously; 2) Designs lightweight two-sided diagonal preconditioners: maintains exponential moving averages (EMA) of row-wise and column-wise squared gradient norms to construct left and right diagonal preconditioning matrices, preserving the matrix structure of parameters with minimal extra overhead; 3) Uses norm grafting to stabilize update scale; 4) MALTER further introduces a scalar step size adapted via Adam-style norm-based noise adjustment in the preconditioned space to improve robustness to stochastic gradient noise. Convergence guarantees for stochastic non-convex optimization are also provided.e:T759,The model continues the Kathleen line. The input side uses a ByteRotate encoder: instead of learning 256 separate byte embeddings, it learns one vector and derives all byte representations through DFT phase rotation. This means the whole byte alphabet is covered with only 128 parameters. Sequence modeling is handled by a multi-scale reverberant state, which replaces attention with three banks of content-gated exponential decay states. The recurrence is s_t = gamma_t s_{t-1} + (1-gamma_t) v_t, where gamma_t depends on content, and the three decay ranges cover short, medium, and long memory. A reverb block is LayerNorm, then reverb in parallel with causal depthwise convolution, then learned gate mixing, then FFN. The trunk has three such blocks with hidden size 128. A notable engineering addition is fast scan: the recurrence is parallelized in chunks of 16 using a cumprod/cumsum closed form, numerically matching the sequential loop to 1e-7 while giving roughly 10–50x wall-clock speedup. That makes the 512 MB training point feasible on free-tier hardware. The baseline is a parameter-matched attention model with the same skeleton, replacing reverb plus convolution by 4-head causal SDPA. Beyond byte-level LM, the paper also uses a 3.08M-parameter word-level variant with a 10K vocabulary for generation studies. Another technical contribution is FORM DISTANCE, a non-parametric, frozen, offline metric for whether sampled text has the statistical form of natural text. It is not a semantic metric. The authors explicitly document one case where the metric was gamed and how they patched it, which makes the evaluation setup more credible than LLM-as-a-judge for this setting. Finally, the decode-time attribution study compares decoding policies, retrieved phrase memory, and several trunk modifications to isolate where generation improvements actually come from.f:T64b,The central experiment is a five-point data-scaling study on WikiText-103 raw UTF-8 bytes, using training subsets of 2, 8, 32, 128, and 512 MB, a fixed 1 MB test slice, 2 epochs, sequence length 256 bytes, and identical AdamW plus cosine recipes for both models. The attention-free reverb model wins at every data point against the parameter-matched transformer baseline. At 512 MB, the reverb model reaches 1.84 bits per byte versus 2.04 for the transformer. At 32 MB, reverb gets 2.011 bpb, while the transformer trained on the full 512 MB still only reaches 2.043 bpb, which the authors summarize as at least a 16x data advantage at equal scale. The log-log slopes are -0.074 for REVERB and -0.107 for ATTN. The paper correctly warns against reading the steeper transformer slope as superiority; it is more consistent with catching up from behind, while both curves are already flattening. Model sizes are 565,888 parameters for the byte-level reverb LM and 463,360 for the attention baseline. The paper also reports replication on a second dataset and a frozen-probe transfer study that supports the same conclusion. The generation-side result is arguably more interesting: at this small scale, text form is driven mainly by decoding policy and retrieved phrases, not by extra architectural additions to the trunk. Four pre-registered trunk additions did not help. That negative result is useful. The main caveat is scope: the advantage is shown in a byte-level, tiny-model, low-budget regime, and should not be read as evidence that attention-free models will dominate at medium or large pretraining scale.10:T491,A good skim follows the question structure. Start with the architecture section to understand exactly what ByteRotate and multi-scale reverb replace, and what inductive bias they introduce. Then read the scaling section carefully, focusing on fairness of parameter matching, identical training recipes, and the full bpb curves rather than a single endpoint. After that, read the FORM DISTANCE section, including the validation and the patch after metric gaming; this part is easy to overlook, but it shows unusual care in generation evaluation. Finish with the decode-time attribution study, especially which trunk modifications failed and which decoding or retrieval settings helped. About 30 minutes should be enough to capture the main message. For a deeper read, Appendix A on fast scan is worth attention because engineering feasibility is central if this family is ever to be tested at larger scale. Two questions should stay in mind while reading: whether the data-efficiency gain partly reflects asymmetry between byte-level modeling and the chosen attention baseline, and whether FORM DISTANCE measures only textual form rather than general generation quality.11:T5f6,SpecDrop is intentionally simple. The model has a shared stem, K parallel modules, and a shared head. Categories are assigned to modules through a fixed assignment matrix, implemented in the simplest case by round-robin mapping from M categories to K modules. During training, for an example with category c, the assigned module is kept with high probability p_a, while all other modules are kept with a small leakage probability p_i. So this is not hard routing; some cross-category gradient sharing remains. The branch outputs are merged using a category-independent fixed denominator S. The paper argues that this denominator makes train and test forward passes match in expectation and keeps the merged activation scale calibrated to the single-branch scale, avoiding magnitude drift from stochastic dropout. An optional always-on shared expert can capture general features. There are no learned routing parameters and no auxiliary load-balancing losses. The point is not that fixed routing is universally better than learned routing. The point is to isolate the variable: if a minimal parameter-free routing scheme already works on clean partitions and fails on fuzzy ones, then the bottleneck is likely partition alignment rather than router design. A strong part of the paper is the information-matched masking control, where the same category label is given to a dense model through output masking. This separates the value of label information itself from the value of turning that information into modular structure.12:T4bb,The experiments span four settings across vision and language. The strongest positive results appear in aligned vision partitions. On CIFAR-100 and ImageNet-1K with superclass labels, SpecDrop clearly outperforms parameter-matched baselines that do not use the category label. The abstract reports +4.75 top-1 over dense on CIFAR-100 and +6.53 over matched No-Routing+SE on ImageNet-1K, while also leading the multi-branch routing baselines evaluated by the authors. At the same time, the information-matched masking control shows that if the only goal is accuracy, directly using the label to mask a dense model can be even stronger. SpecDrop’s contribution is therefore not superior label usage per se, but the induction of trained modular structure. The language results are the more important constraint. On fuzzy partitions in SlimPajama-6B language modeling and SuperNI/LoRA instruction tuning over Llama-3.2-1B, SpecDrop is essentially tied with matched-architecture baselines within seed noise. That negative result is central: it limits how far the method can be extrapolated to LLM pretraining and supports the paper’s claim that partition clarity, not router complexity, is the binding condition.13:Ta78,方法核心是一个 exact speculative rollout engine，名字叫 SpecRoll。它由四个部件组成。

第一，proposal family 不是独立 drafter，而是挂在 target backbone hidden state 上的多 horizon future-token heads，形式上接近 MEDUSA。给定前缀 x_{<=t}，target 先精确采样一个 anchor token x_{t+1}，然后多个 head 并行预测后续若干位置的 token，复用 target 的词表投影矩阵 W。这些候选组成一个 prefix-closed sparse tree。这样做的好处是省掉第二个 AR backbone 和对应 KV cache，proposal 成本更低，也减少了 target/drafter 失配来源。

第二，verification 是 exact 的，而且是 concurrency-aware 的。RL rollout 里同时活跃的样本数会随着部分样本提前结束而下降，因此固定大小的 speculative tree 在高并发阶段可能浪费验证算力，在低并发阶段又吃不满硬件。SpecRoll 会根据当前 active concurrency 动态调整 sparse tree 的预算：并发高时保守，减少额外验证负担；并发下降后扩大 tree，换取更高 accepted progress。论文的出发点不是单纯提高单条序列 acceptance，而是优化整个 GRPO rollout 阶段的吞吐。

第三，快路径是 Reflex。它利用“延迟 verifier 反馈在同一轨迹邻近位置上仍有预测性”这一观察，对 proposal hidden state 做 bounded、trajectory-local、gradient-free correction。这里的关键不是学习一个新模型，而是把已经成熟的 verifier error 当作局部校正信号，在不反传的情况下修正后续 proposal。为了避免误修正，作者加了 conservative alignment gate：只有当过去误差对未来仍有可预测性时才启用；同时还有 candidate-boundary safeguard，防止在候选边界附近做有害干预。这个设计试图区分 transient local mismatch 和 persistent drift：前者不值得动参数，局部修一下就够；后者才交给慢路径。

第四，慢路径只在 sustained degradation 被检测到时，才更新 future-token heads 的参数。也就是说，SpecRoll 不把所有失配都转成在线学习问题，而是先用 Reflex 吃掉短期、局部、轨迹内的偏差，把真正跨轨迹持续存在的 proposal 退化留给参数更新。这一点和 FastGRPO 的持续在线训练独立 drafter 不同，后者把适应主要放在参数层，代价更高，也不区分误差类型。

从训练目标上，SpecRoll 始终保持 exact target verification 为最终裁决，因此 committed token 的分布不变，GRPO 的 reward、advantage 和优化目标都不被改写。它优化的是 rollout 生成过程，而不是 RL objective 本身。14:T96f,SpecRoll is an exact speculative rollout engine built from four main pieces.

First, the proposer is not a separate drafter model. It uses MEDUSA-style future-token heads attached to the target backbone hidden state. Given a prefix, the target model samples an exact anchor token x_{t+1}, and multiple horizon heads predict later positions in parallel, reusing the target vocabulary projection. These candidates form a prefix-closed sparse tree. This removes the need for a second autoregressive backbone and its KV cache, reducing proposal cost and simplifying synchronization.

Second, verification remains exact and is made concurrency-aware. In GRPO, the number of active rollouts changes over time because some responses finish earlier than others. A fixed speculative tree is therefore inefficient: too expensive when concurrency is high, and too small to saturate hardware when concurrency drops. SpecRoll adjusts the sparse-tree budget based on active concurrency, using a smaller budget early and a larger one later. The objective is not just better acceptance on a single sequence, but better throughput for the whole rollout stage.

Third, the fast adaptation path is Reflex. The key observation is that delayed verifier errors can still be predictive for nearby future positions within the same trajectory. Reflex converts such mature verifier feedback into bounded, trajectory-local hidden-state corrections without backpropagation. It is guarded by a conservative alignment gate, which activates only when past errors are predictive of future mismatch, and by candidate-boundary safeguards that suppress harmful interventions near unstable boundaries. The design explicitly separates transient local mismatch from persistent drift.

Fourth, the slow path updates the future-token heads only when sustained degradation is detected. In other words, SpecRoll does not treat every mismatch as an online learning problem. It first uses Reflex to absorb short-lived, local errors, and only escalates recurring cross-trajectory mismatch into parameter updates. This differs from FastGRPO, where adaptation relies more heavily on continuously training a separate drafter.

Throughout the method, exact target verification is authoritative. The committed-token distribution is unchanged, so rewards, advantages, and the GRPO objective remain intact. The method changes rollout efficiency, not the RL objective.15:T5f5,The evaluation covers five models from 1.5B to 14B parameters and three math reasoning datasets, for 15 matched model-dataset settings. Against vanilla GRPO, SpecRoll reports 1.26x to 2.15x generation speedup and 1.21x to 2.04x end-to-end speedup. Against FastGRPO, it is better on both generation time and end-to-end time in all 15 matched settings, with an average pairwise end-to-end gain of 1.18x.

These results matter for two reasons. First, the gains are not limited to token-generation microbenchmarks; they carry through to the full RL training loop. Second, the combination of lightweight proposal heads and two-timescale adaptation appears more cost-effective than maintaining a separately trained online drafter, at least in the tested regime.

The ablations show that both the fast path and the slow path help individually, and the combination works best. That means Reflex is not a cosmetic add-on; it absorbs a meaningful portion of transient mismatch. At the same time, Reflex alone is insufficient because policy drift accumulates across trajectories, so slow parameter updates are still needed. Another useful finding is the role of the concurrency-aware tree budget, which explains why speculative decoding settings from ordinary inference do not transfer directly to RL rollouts.

The paper focuses on wall-clock and end-to-end efficiency rather than final reward improvements, which is consistent with the exact-verification design: the training signal should remain unchanged, while throughput improves.16:T5a1,This is a skim paper overall, but it is worth a closer read if you work on RL systems or post-training infrastructure.

Start with the introduction and isolate the main design choice relative to FastGRPO: instead of training a better standalone drafter, SpecRoll splits adaptation into local gradient-free correction and infrequent parameter updates. Then read Figure 2 and the method section around the proposal engine to understand the interaction among future-token heads, the anchor token, the sparse tree, and exact verification.

Next, focus on the Reflex section. The main questions are not the algebra but the mechanism: why delayed verifier errors remain useful locally, how the alignment gate decides when to intervene, and why candidate-boundary safeguards are necessary. The interesting systems idea is that the method exploits short-range error correlation inside a rollout trajectory.

In the experiments, prioritize the main timing table and the ablations. Check three things in the main results: gains over vanilla GRPO, pairwise wins over FastGRPO, and stability across model scales. In the ablations, look at what happens when the fast path, slow path, or concurrency-aware budget is removed.

If time is limited, 25 to 35 minutes is enough to capture the main idea. For implementation or reproduction, the most important details are the Reflex trigger conditions, the slow-path update schedule, and the tree-budget policy.17:T479,The proposed method is NOVA-KV (Non-Orthogonal Vector-quantized Attention for KV cache). The authors formulate KV compression as a rate-distortion problem where distortion is error in attention products, split into a key-side term from q·k inner products and a value-side term from attention-weighted values. Under a high-resolution assumption they derive closed-form optimal transforms: for values the optimal transform is orthogonal and recovers the covariance-based transform used heuristically in prior work such as Oscar; for keys the optimal transform is non-orthogonal and satisfies a generalized Parseval relation, meaning MSE in the transform domain equals key-query inner-product distortion in the original domain. This reduces quantizer design to a standard MSE problem. On top of that, NOVA-KV applies vector quantization to key coefficients and groups entries so that group volumes are approximately equalized; this allows equal-size codebooks to approach the variable-rate distortion bound while preserving a fixed-width layout required by serving engines. The method is integrated into SGLang with a fused Triton decoding kernel.18:T664,A fully crossed controlled experiment is used to isolate backend effects, with all nuisance variables held fixed: a single RTX 3090 GPU, fp16 weight precision, and a unified evaluation protocol. Four dimensions are systematically varied: 1. Five backends, ordered by increasing isolation from the raw PyTorch compute graph: hf_raw (direct call to AutoModelForCausalLM.generate() with the model's official chat template, used as the reference baseline), hf_pipeline (the exact same in-memory model object wrapped in HuggingFace's text-generation pipeline abstraction), langchain_hf (the same pipeline wrapped in LangChain's orchestration layer), vLLM 0.8.5 V0 engine (separate CUDA context, independent PagedAttention, decoding, and sampling implementations, running on identical SafeTensors weights as the reference), and Ollama (standalone OS process running llama.cpp, using fp16 GGUF weights, independent tokenizer and runtime stack). The first four backends run on numerically identical weight matrices, so divergence between them cannot be attributed to weight variation; GGUF conversion artifacts for Ollama are treated as part of the practical backend effect rather than a removed confound. 2. Three ~1B parameter instruction-tuned models: Llama-3.2-1B-Inst, Qwen2.5-1.5B-Inst, Gemma-3-1B-IT. The 1B scale is chosen to enable exhaustive replication across all condition combinations at manageable compute cost. 3. Six benchmarks covering both factual knowledge and social bias tasks. 4. Four generation modes, ranging from deterministic greedy decoding to stochastic sampling, that progressively introduce sources of variability.19:T4de,Each instance is formalized as x=(s,q,E,R): a skill s=(m,p) with metadata m (name/short description/path) and full procedure p; a user task q; an executable environment E (Docker sandbox with tools/files); and a hidden scoring rubric R applied to the full interaction trajectory.
The key constraint is progressive disclosure: the agent starts with (q,E,m) and must decide to retrieve the skill file to access p.
Metrics are decomposed into:
- Trigger: whether the agent retrieves the relevant skill given only metadata.
- Compliance: whether it follows the procedure after retrieval (procedure-level adherence, not output formatting).
- Boundary: whether it avoids explicitly forbidden operations/shortcuts.
A gated aggregate SU score credits execution only if Trigger succeeds, preventing “accidental success without using the skill” from inflating results.
Benchmark construction emphasizes verifiability: real skills curated from community repositories; tasks designed to leave observable traces; tempting but forbidden shortcuts to stress Boundary; trajectory-based rubrics; end-to-end validation; plus masked-skill screening to remove overly generic requirements and multi-agent adversarial review to audit scope and rubric checkability.1a:T585,The paper studies three families of confidence estimation for LLM classifiers:
- Verbalization-based: prompt the LLM to output a numeric confidence (with variants like CoT/self-probing/top-k verbalization).
- Sampling-based: sample multiple predictions and aggregate (e.g., consistency frequency; mean of sampled verbalized confidences).
- Logit-based: use token log-probabilities (e.g., probability of the class-label token) when the API exposes logprobs.
Main analysis: verbalization yields highly discrete confidence values due to strong preferences for certain numeric tokens, producing only a few distinct thresholds.
Evaluation pitfall: AUARC requires integrating a curve defined by discrete operating points. Linear interpolation effectively invents intermediate operating points, which can over-credit sparse methods; stepwise interpolation better matches the semantics of thresholding (metrics should not change until the threshold crosses an actually attainable confidence value). The authors advocate standardizing stepwise interpolation.
Proposed method: verbalization logprobs. Instead of treating the single generated confidence number as the estimate, it uses the token probabilities (logprobs) of the verbalized digits/values to compute a probability-weighted (continuous) confidence score, reducing sparsity without additional sampling cost (assuming logprobs are available for generated tokens).1b:Tce6,方法上，作者采用的是一个 decoder-only、Llama-3 风格的统一骨干，并使用 Transfusion 框架把文本的离散 next-token prediction 与图像生成的连续 flow matching 放进同一模型。默认控制实验的主干是 1.5B Llama-like backbone，但由于为 text/image token 设置了 modality-specific split FFN，总参数约 2.3B；大规模验证则使用 13.5B MoE，在 2T token 上训练。文本 tokenizer 使用 Llama-3 BPE，视觉侧支持多种 visual tokenizer，以避免结论只依赖某一种图像离散化方式。

技术贡献可以拆成四块。

第一块是 knowledge flow。作者先在真实数据上看语言、视觉理解、视觉生成之间的迁移，再在严格控制的合成环境里做因果剥离。这里的关键不是简单比较多任务训练是否涨点，而是看哪一种能力会给另一种能力提供可迁移表征，以及这种迁移是否对称。结论是明显不对称：语言对视觉理解、视觉生成的帮助方式不同；视觉生成与视觉理解之间也不是天然互利，而是依赖概念类型。作者用合成数据把概念级 transfer 拆出来，这一点比只在自然图文语料上做 ablation 更干净，因为后者很容易被数据共现和任务难度混淆。

第二块是 synergy vs. competition。作者把“模态是否互相帮助”追到数据复杂度和网络结构两个层面。数据层面，过于复杂的语言或视觉任务会让模态争夺容量；相反，适度简化的一侧可以给另一侧提供辅助监督，形成协同。结构层面，作者分析 Transformer 内部哪些部位更适合共享，哪些部位更适合隔离。结论是 shared attention 更有利于跨模态信息交换，因为注意力负责 token 间路由；而 separate FFN 更适合隔离模态特有变换，减少在参数空间里的直接冲突。这个设计判断很像把 attention 视作通信层，把 FFN 视作模态私有计算层。作者还验证这种现象在不同 visual tokenizer 下都成立，说明不是某个图像表示偶然带来的结果。

第三块是 early unification。作者系统改变视觉数据引入的时间点，以及模态训练的调度方式，比较从一开始 joint training、先文本后视觉、分阶段 curriculum 等方案。核心发现是视觉必须尽早进入，而且要与语言同时共训练。若先把语言模型训得很强，再做视觉接入，模型会出现作者称为 vision laziness 的现象：优化更倾向复用已有语言先验，而不是认真更新视觉相关子网络，导致视觉表征学习不足。这一点对当前很多“先训 LLM、再补视觉”的路线是直接挑战，因为它说明后接视觉不只是效率问题，而是会改变最优解的形态。

第四块是 recipes。作者把前面三类发现合并成可执行 recipe：采用不对称的数据混合比例，使用 shared attention + split FFN 一类的参数共享设计，并坚持早期统一、同步联合训练。然后在大规模 MoE 训练中做单变量对照，验证这些设计在更大参数和更长训练下仍然成立。这里的价值在于，它不是提出一个新模块，而是把多模态预训练里最容易拍脑袋决定的几件事，变成了有实验支撑的默认选择。1c:Ta3c,The model is a decoder-only Llama-3-style backbone trained under the Transfusion framework, where text uses discrete next-token prediction and image generation uses continuous flow matching inside one unified model. The default controlled setup uses a 1.5B Llama-like backbone, but with modality-specific split FFNs the total parameter count is about 2.3B. The large-scale validation uses a 13.5B MoE model trained on 2T tokens. Text uses the Llama-3 BPE tokenizer, and the study supports multiple visual tokenizers so that the conclusions are not tied to one image representation.

The technical contribution is organized into four parts.

1) Knowledge flow. The authors first measure transfer among language, visual understanding, and visual generation on real-world data, then move to synthetic controlled environments to remove confounders. The key point is not just whether multitask training helps, but which capability transfers to which other capability, and whether the transfer is symmetric. Their answer is no: transfer is strongly asymmetric, and the interaction between visual understanding and visual generation is concept-dependent rather than universally positive.

2) Synergy vs. competition. The paper traces modality interaction to both data complexity and network structure. On the data side, simplified tasks in one modality can help the other modality, while high complexity can create competition for capacity. On the architecture side, the authors analyze where sharing helps and where separation is safer. Shared attention appears to support cross-modal routing and communication, while separate FFNs reduce destructive interference by keeping modality-specific transformations private. This is a useful architectural decomposition: attention as the communication layer, FFN as the modality-specialized computation layer.

3) Early unification. The paper varies when vision is introduced and how modalities are scheduled during training. The main result is that vision should be present early and trained jointly with language, not added after a strong language prior has already formed. Late introduction leads to what the authors call “vision laziness”: the model leans on existing language priors and under-optimizes the vision-related components. This is an important challenge to the common retrofit paradigm.

4) Recipes. The final recipe combines asymmetric data mixing, selective parameter sharing such as shared attention plus split FFNs, and early simultaneous multimodal training. The paper then validates these choices with controlled single-variable comparisons at larger scale.1d:T6d1,The experiments operate at two levels. The first level is controlled mechanism analysis. The default model is a 16-layer decoder-only Transformer with hidden size 2048, GQA 32/8, SwiGLU, RoPE, QK-norm, and FlashAttention. The backbone is 1.5B, and the split-FFN multimodal version totals about 2.3B parameters. Using both real and synthetic data, the paper studies transfer among language, visual understanding, and visual generation, then runs systematic ablations over task complexity, parameter sharing, visual tokenizer choice, vision introduction timing, and training schedule. The main findings reported in the text are: transfer is not symmetric; visual understanding and generation interact differently depending on concept type; modality synergy versus competition is largely controlled by data complexity; shared attention plus separate FFNs is a strong structural compromise; and late alignment induces vision laziness.

The second level is large-scale recipe validation. The authors train 13.5B MoE models on 2T tokens and run controlled single-variable comparisons to test whether the earlier findings survive scale. The excerpt provided here does not include all result tables, so exact benchmark numbers are incomplete, but the paper clearly positions the large-scale section as a scaling check rather than a small-model anecdote. When reading the full paper, the most important numbers to extract are: the trade-off curves under different data mixtures; the gains or losses from different sharing patterns such as shared attention versus split FFNs; and the gap between early joint training and late or staged introduction of vision, ideally together with optimization diagnostics that support the vision-laziness interpretation.1e:T641,A good reading order is problem, mechanism, then recipe.

Start with the introduction and section openings to lock in the four claims. The paper is not about standard VLM alignment; it is about unified multimodal pretraining where language, visual understanding, and visual generation are trained together.

Then read Section 3 and Section 5 carefully. Section 3 is the center of the paper because the knowledge-flow analysis motivates the later data-mixing recipe. Pay attention to how real-data experiments and synthetic experiments play different roles: one gives external realism, the other gives cleaner causal interpretation. Do not only look at average scores; check whether transfer is directional and concept-specific. Section 5 matters because it directly informs training strategy. Focus on how the paper defines and diagnoses vision laziness, and whether the evidence comes from gradients, parameter updates, module utilization, or recovery curves.

After that, read Section 4 and Section 6. In Section 4, the most useful takeaway is the shared-attention / separate-FFN design logic. It is worth comparing mentally against alternatives such as full sharing, cross-attention-only fusion, or modality-specific experts. In Section 6, check whether the large-scale MoE results preserve the same trends and whether the single-variable controls are actually clean.

If time is limited, prioritize: the four headline figures, the synthetic knowledge-flow experiments, the early-unification timing comparisons, and the large-scale recipe tables. A careful read should take around 1.5 to 2.5 hours.1f:T6ae,The study uses strictly controlled experiments with identical SDPO code, teacher implementation, and clipping mechanisms, varying only task difficulty to rule out implementation differences. It then proposes and validates a full causal chain of failure, with all five links measured on the same set of Qwen3-8B runs: 1) PI bias: the teacher encodes the surface form of a single reference solution rather than correctness. The PI Bias Score (teacher-minus-student log-probability ratio) reaches 0.52 for the in-context solution but ≤0.02 for other correct solutions to the same problem, barely distinguishing them from unrelated solutions. 2) Loss decoupled from correctness: split by verifier outcome, per-token loss (≈3.5×10⁻⁴) and KL (≈0.03) overlap at every step for correct and incorrect rollouts, and even run in reverse — the teacher-student gap is 0.7 on correct rollouts vs 0.45 on incorrect ones, meaning learning pressure concentrates where the student is already right. 3) Density diffusion: stopwords, punctuation, and whitespace absorb 55.4% of per-token loss, while content words, numbers, and math symbols that determine the answer account for much less. 4) Exploration penalty: within correct rollouts, positions off the reference path have KL≈0.31 vs ≈0.08 on-path, a 4x difference. The teacher cannot distinguish productive detours from errors, suppressing the search required for reasoning and shortening responses. 5) Student flattening instead of sharpening: the teacher-student gap closes from ≈3.1 to ≈0, but the ±1 standard deviation band does not contract, and student entropy rises, resulting in a flatter, earlier-committing policy that solves no more problems.20:T783,The contribution has three layers. First, the collection itself: Reasoning Core contains 50 generators spanning math, logic, planning, state tracking, formal languages, structured data, games, causality, and code. The authors aim for semantic breadth rather than surface diversity. Many tasks vary expression trees, logical forms, program structure, or formal systems through recursive or grammar-based generation. Planning and game tasks also randomize the underlying domain, action schemas, and transition rules instead of repeatedly instantiating a fixed environment. Second, the SFT-oriented representation design. Problem metadata is separated from prompt rendering, and the training prompts use zero-shot instructions rather than few-shot demonstrations to preserve context budget for more independent samples. More importantly, targets are canonicalized whenever possible. For sets, plans, proofs, and programs, the training target is forced into a deterministic serialization, such as lexicographic ordering, so autoregressive supervision does not waste capacity on arbitrary but equivalent prefixes. The answers are also kept compact: numbers, labels, short expressions, proof-line indices, plans, or short programs are preferred over long explanations. Third, semantic scoring and auditing. Training uses one canonical target, but evaluation accepts any semantically valid answer: sets are order-invariant, lambda terms are compared up to alpha-renaming, plans are executed, proofs are compiled, and programs are tested. The paper also includes a repository-scale semantic audit using model-assisted review, API sampling, manual adjudication, and regression tests to expose subtle generator, target, and scorer failures in external collections. A useful conceptual distinction throughout the paper is between semantic validity and training utility: a problem can be well-defined yet still be poor supervised data.21:T4ef,The experimental emphasis is controlled comparison rather than isolated benchmark wins. Reasoning Core is compared against Procedural Warmup, Reasoning Gym, and SynLogic under a matched completion-supervised protocol, across four base-model settings and multiple training durations. The headline result in the abstract is that, in the main 3B setting, Reasoning Core achieves the best mean performance on DROP, LogiQA, and ARC-Challenge, outperforming both the no-procedural-data baseline and all three alternative procedural collections. The paper argues that the gain comes from data design rather than simply having more tasks: many external collections were built for RL, with more complex answer formats, weaker canonicalization, and lower token efficiency for SFT. Additional diagnostics include free-generation behavior, retention, and repository audits, which help separate immediate gains from forgetting and from latent semantic defects in the data source. Even without all tables here, the qualitative findings are clear: broad procedural mixtures can transfer under completion-supervised training; verifiability alone does not predict usefulness; and semantic bugs in public procedural repositories are common enough to matter for supervised training.22:T8d9,方法叫 Recoverability-Aware Intervention Learning，简称 RAIL。核心概念是 recoverability。按作者的定义，recoverability 衡量的是：在某个任务或轨迹状态上施加 intervention 后，reward-distribution signal 能提升多少。这里的重点不是最终 reward 本身，而是干预是否让训练看到更有区分度、更有学习价值的回报分布。

围绕这个概念，RAIL 做了三层设计。

第一，它把 rollout intervention 建模成一个 online contextual bandit。context 是当前任务或轨迹状态，action 是一个结构化 intervention 选择，observed utility 是实际观测到的 recoverability gain。这样做的意义是，controller 学到的是“在当前 policy、当前状态分布下，什么干预最能带来额外学习信号”，而不是依赖固定规则。作者明确把这一步用来弥补 non-stationary gap：policy 在变，干预策略也应跟着学。

第二，action space 不是单一的 rollout 数量，而是 structured intervention space。虽然给出的全文片段没有展开全部细节，但从引言和方法定义看，至少包括在哪里 branch、采用哪种 intervention strategy 等耦合决策。作者把这一步用来弥补 non-scalar gap：很多有价值的干预不是“多采样几个”这么简单，而是要在正确的状态、以正确的方式扩展轨迹。

第三，部署方式采用 shadow-to-live procedure。先用一个短 shadow phase 收集 intervention traces 和 supervision，不直接让 learned controller 影响主训练；等 controller 对 utility 有一定把握后，再进入 utility-gated live phase，选择性地把它接入 policy optimization。这个设计比较务实，因为在线学 intervention policy 容易把训练搞偏，先 shadow 再 live 相当于给 controller 一个冷启动缓冲区，也降低了早期错误干预的代价。

从整体上看，RAIL 不是改 GRPO 的 policy gradient 公式，而是改 rollout generation process。它把 rollout 生成从固定程序变成可学习、可适应的训练环节。和基于 entropy、variance 的启发式 branching 方法相比，差别在于：RAIL 关心的是 intervention 的 realized utility，而不是某个代理指标是否高。23:T7cf,RAIL stands for Recoverability-Aware Intervention Learning. Its central concept is recoverability, defined as how much an intervention improves the reward-distribution signal at a task or trajectory state. The emphasis is not just on final reward, but on whether the intervention exposes more informative reward contrast for learning.

The method has three main layers.

First, rollout intervention is formulated as an online contextual bandit. The context is the current task or trajectory state, the action is a structured intervention choice, and the observed utility is the realized recoverability gain. This directly addresses the non-stationary gap: as the policy evolves, the intervention policy should also adapt from observed outcomes rather than remain a fixed heuristic.

Second, the action space is structured rather than scalar. From the paper’s framing, it includes coupled decisions such as where to branch and which intervention strategy to apply, not merely how many additional rollouts to generate. This addresses the non-scalar gap: useful intervention often depends on acting at the right state with the right branching pattern, not just increasing sample count.

Third, deployment uses a shadow-to-live procedure. A short shadow phase collects intervention traces and supervision without directly affecting the main optimization loop. Once the controller has enough signal, a utility-gated live phase selectively applies it during policy optimization. This is a practical design choice because online intervention learning can destabilize training if deployed too early; the shadow phase acts as a cold-start buffer.

Overall, RAIL does not modify the GRPO gradient formula itself. It modifies the rollout-generation process, turning it from a fixed procedure into an adaptive, learned component of training. Relative to entropy- or variance-based branching heuristics, the key difference is that RAIL optimizes for realized intervention utility rather than a proxy statistic.24:T4cf,The paper states that it evaluates RAIL along four axes: effectiveness, adaptivity, expressiveness, and efficiency, and that it outperforms heuristic intervention baselines across multiple benchmarks. However, the provided full-text excerpt does not include the detailed result tables, model sizes, dataset sizes, or exact gains, so those numbers cannot be reproduced reliably here.

What can be inferred from the text is still useful. First, the learned recoverability controller appears to adapt better than static heuristics as the policy changes, which supports the claim that the non-stationary gap is real. Second, structured intervention outperforms scalar budget control, supporting the non-scalar argument. Third, the shadow-to-live mechanism is presented as an important stability device rather than a cosmetic engineering detail.

When reading the full paper, the most important experimental checks are: whether recoverability gain correlates consistently with downstream training improvement; how the controller’s decisions evolve over the course of training; and whether the controller overhead is offset by rollout savings relative to heuristic baselines. Those points determine how convincing the method really is.25:T539,This is best treated as a skim paper. Focus on the problem formulation and the training-loop design rather than every bandit detail on the first pass.

Start with the introduction and the section that defines recoverability. The key question is whether recoverability is genuinely an outcome-based utility measure or just a relabeling of familiar proxies like entropy or reward variance. That distinction determines the paper’s value.

Then read the method section around the contextual-bandit formulation and the structured intervention space. Pay attention to what decisions are actually included in the action space and what features the controller observes. After that, read the shadow-to-live deployment procedure carefully, because that is what makes the method plausible in a real online training loop.

In the experiments, prioritize the four evaluation axes. Effectiveness tells you whether the method improves training. Adaptivity tells you whether it remains useful as the policy changes. Expressiveness tells you whether structured actions matter beyond scalar budgets. Efficiency tells you whether the controller overhead is justified.

If the goal is triage, 20 to 30 minutes is enough. For implementation, you would need the appendix details on the intervention space, the utility definition, and the online update rule.26:T447,The central notion is gauge equivariance: an optimizer is equivariant if it behaves consistently under orthogonal rotations of the latent factor basis. A structure theorem shows that a memoryless update is equivariant iff it takes the form Δ=H(GG^T)G, a left preconditioner determined by the Gram matrix. Under this classification, GD, Polyak momentum, Adam with a shared scalar second moment, Muon (including its Newton–Schulz iterates), and Shampoo's Kronecker preconditioner with damping are equivariant; Adam, RMSProp, signSGD, Lion, and Adafactor break equivariance because they apply entrywise nonlinearities at zero state. An exact first-step defect is derived for zero-initialized Adam. A transfer theorem shows that dynamics under a shared scalar preconditioner equal gradient flow under a rescaled time, so existing low-rank/minimum-nuclear-norm results for gradient flow carry over. Equivariance is necessary but not sufficient: low-rank convergence also depends on the spectral schedule over singular values attracting dynamics to the low-rank manifold within the training budget.27:T41b,The main evaluation runs on the full Python slice of SWE-bench Pro (266 tasks) under the official capped-budget protocol. SuperScout resolves 159/266 tasks, slightly above the best single fixer's 158/266, at a total cost per solve of $0.230 versus $1.274 for the strongest model — roughly a 5x reduction. The searcher's total GPU cost across the evaluation is only $1.13, negligible in the overall budget. A pre-hoc audit replays published per-task outcomes on three SWE benchmarks: solve-set containment ranges from 0.773 to 0.941 across model pairs, and 0.90–0.93 even among frontier-only pools, confirming heavy nesting. None of the learned routers tested (k-NN, logistic, MLP, clustering, language-rule baselines) reliably beats always calling the strongest model. A paired calibration study on 100 fresh tasks shows the handoff redistributes rather than adds solving ability: it lifts the three cheaper fixers while slightly hurting the strongest, and hidden-state features improve cost-aware routing, whereas the handoff text alone does not.28:T5e9,The method has three main components. First is the milestone-plan-act workflow. Instead of planning at the finest step granularity from the start, the orchestrator first decomposes a complex query into milestones. This reduces low-level execution noise and gives the policy a more stable state abstraction. Within each milestone, it builds a dependency-aware execution graph and can run independent steps in parallel across executors. Second is explicit executor profiling. Each executor comes with textual descriptions of capability and cost, so the orchestrator reasons jointly over task requirements and executor profiles rather than routing by query features alone. This differs from standard learned routers that often rely on shallow signals and fixed executor sets. Third is end-to-end RL with tree-structured rollout. Starting from an initial trajectory, the system expands selected milestone or plan nodes into alternative branches, producing multiple strategies under comparable contexts. Rewards combine task correctness, execution efficiency, and trajectory completeness. The completeness term is important because otherwise the policy may exploit partial execution or early stopping. Rewards are normalized at both node and query levels to make signals more comparable across branches and tasks and to reduce variance. Compared with standard single-trajectory RL, this branch-based exploration gives denser supervision around local decision points and is better aligned with multi-step orchestration.29:T407,The experiments cover three task families: mathematical reasoning, embodied decision making, and deep research. The main claim is consistent improvement on the performance-efficiency frontier over prior agentic baselines. The key result is not simply higher success rate, but better task completion at the same or lower cost, or lower executor cost at comparable performance. The paper also emphasizes generalization to unseen executors, which is more informative than evaluations on a fixed executor pool. The provided full-content excerpt does not include the final tables with exact numbers, model sizes, training steps, or cost reductions, so it would be unsafe to invent precise metrics. Still, the experimental structure makes clear what to inspect: how efficiency is measured, whether cost is token-based or latency-based, how deep the rollout tree goes, whether reward normalization stabilizes training, and what “unseen executor” means operationally—new profiles, swapped capabilities, or newly introduced executors.2a:T4ed,Argus structures a long campaign as a sequence of bounded missions over a durable project state. Four model-driven roles split authority: a Manager anchors intent and campaign state, a Planner selects the next unit of work, an Engineer implements and evaluates it, and a Reviewer independently inspects artifacts before issuing completion verdicts; low-risk tasks may use recorded Engineer self-review. These roles operate across three planes—control, execution, and records—so scheduling, execution, and bookkeeping stay separable. The working contract is written explicitly as K_t=(ι, o_t, c_t, v_t), where ι is the invariant user intent, o_t the current operational objective, c_t the constraints, and v_t the verification criteria. Material updates to the objective, constraints, or verifier require evidence, admission by the authorized role, and durable logging; an accepted update counts as compounding only when it reduces future task risk or cost. The mechanism is called verification-gated fixed-model runtime self-evolution: model parameters stay fixed, but later missions start from an evolved search policy with retained routes, measurements, verdicts, reusable skills, verifiers, and routing decisions rather than merely a longer transcript.2b:T48c,The audit performs training-free interventions exactly at the relay boundary of released systems. Cache geometry (layer count, sequence length, dtype) is held fixed across arms, and only the content is replaced. Six arms are defined: True (cache from the paired example), Deranged (cache from a different example in the same batch via a fixed-point-free permutation, preserving the marginal cache distribution exactly), Zero (all entries zeroed), Random (Gaussian entries moment-matched per example, layer, and K/V tensor to the true cache), Intact-no-private (sender processes the example with private facts replaced by irrelevant counterparts, used only in the calibrated regime), and Direct-text reference (private information passed verbatim to the receiver). Tasks are split into two regimes: a calibrated regime with procedurally generated sender-private facts unrecoverable by the receiver, and a natural regime using standard benchmarks (GSM8K, ARC-Challenge, MedQA) where the receiver can solve alone. The key contrast is True minus Deranged — the "example-pairing term" — which is the necessary condition for the "latent content is used" attribution.2c:T4f1,In the calibrated regime, across five checkpoints from three model families (Qwen3-4B/8B/14B, Mistral-Nemo-12B, phi-4) and a prose counterfactual QA surface, the True arm reaches near 100% accuracy versus 23–25% for Deranged / answer-irrelevant arms, showing the audit instrument reads at ceiling where it should. In the natural regime, under a pre-registered five-seed protocol with Holm-corrected equivalence testing, the True-versus-Deranged gap is bounded within ±2.8 points (a margin anchored to the audited system's own reported gain) on GSM8K and ARC-Challenge across three Qwen3 scales, and on MedQA at 8B; only one cell shows a small detected advantage inside the margin, and a second model family shows no detected pairing advantage at all. Notably, in one natural-regime cell the Zero arm trails True by 14.7 points while Deranged is indistinguishable from True — meaning there is a generic benefit from having any cache, but no example-specific content transfer. ","starred":true,"priority":"p1","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-08-05T03:23:31Z","category_id":"llm_pretrain","cover_url":"/covers/2608_04407.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.AI","cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇论文解决的是 MoE 预训练里低状态矩阵优化器失效的问题。Sinkhorn 类优化器对 dense Transformer 矩阵有效，因为它可以用当前梯度做行列归一化，省掉 AdamW 的一阶、二阶动量状态。但 MoE expert 矩阵虽然也是二维矩阵，梯度来源却不同：每个 expert 只接收 router 选中的 token 子集，且 gate 权重和 token 分布随训练变化。直接把 dense 模型里的 SAGE/Sinkhorn recipe 搬到 MoE，会把高方差、条件采样的 expert 梯度逐步归一化，导致更新噪声被放大。论文的核心诊断是：MoE expert 需要先做时间平均，再做 Sinkhorn 归一化，而不是对每步瞬时梯度归一化后再隐式平均。","importance":"这篇工作对预训练研究的价值在于，它把“低 optimizer state 能否用于 MoE 预训练”这个问题拆得比较清楚。MoE 的主要吸引力是扩大参数量而保持每 token 计算量可控，但 optimizer state 会重新变成显存瓶颈。AdamW 对每个参数保存两个状态张量，expert 参数越多，这个成本越难接受。论文显示，不能只按张量形状决定优化器策略；expert matrix 和 dense MLP matrix 的梯度统计结构不同。这个结论对大规模 MoE recipe 有直接启发：优化器设计要显式考虑 routing 造成的条件分布漂移。贡献量级偏工程研究，但问题抓得准，负结果也有信息量。它不是提出一个通用替代 AdamW 的完整方案，而是在 MoE expert 这一关键子结构上给出可解释、可复现实验诊断。","technical_approach":"方法从 Sinkhorn optimizer 的失败机制入手。普通 Sinkhorn update 对矩阵梯度 G_t 做多轮行范数、列范数归一化，再乘以全局 scale，优点是 dense matrix 不需要存一阶或二阶状态。SAGE 式 hybrid 通常会把 embedding、vocab 这类难处理张量交给轻状态优化器，把 dense 二维矩阵交给 Sinkhorn。论文发现这个角色划分在 MoE 上不成立，因为 routed expert 的梯度 G_{e,t} 来自 p(x | e in top-k(x,t))，不是完整 mini-batch 分布下的稳定估计。由于 Sinkhorn 是非线性算子，Sinkhorn(E[G_t]) 和 E[Sinkhorn(G_t)] 不等价。对 expert 来说，normalize-then-average 会放大 routing noise；average-then-normalize 更接近 AdamW 一阶动量的作用。MESH 的做法是在 expert 上维护隐藏一阶动量 H_t，然后对 H_t 做 Sinkhorn 归一化。它保留 SAGE/Sinkhorn 对 vocab 和非 expert 矩阵的处理，只替换 expert update。关键实现点是 expert first moment 通过 gradient-buffer lifecycle 维护，而不是作为显式 optimizer-state tensor 保存，因此目标仍是降低 optimizer memory。MESH-B 进一步加入粗粒度 block/neuron inverse-RMS multiplier，相当于给 expert update 增加有限的二阶尺度信息。论文对这个扩展的态度比较谨慎：block 级自适应有帮助，尤其在 seed-42 的诊断里更明显，但多 seed 结果显示一阶时间平滑才是主要因子。","experiments":"实验使用一个受控的 110M 参数 nanowhale DeepSeek-style MoE 预训练设置。直接使用 SAGE/Sinkhorn hybrid 可以把 optimizer state 从 0.883GB 降到 0.331GB，但 eval loss 到 3.8265，明显差于同一设置下 AdamW baseline 的 3.58–3.64。论文把问题定位到 routed expert matrix：对非 expert dense matrix 使用 Sinkhorn 不是主要问题，expert 的条件梯度才是主要失效点。若把 SAGE 应到 expert 上，效果更差；单独降低 Sinkhorn scale、把 expert tensor 融合后做 Sinkhorn、按 route confidence damping，都不能补上差距。负向消融还覆盖了 second-moment-only、sign/momentum-only、confidence-only、scale-only、fused-Sinkhorn 等解释，结论是这些都不足以解释 AdamW 与 stateless Sinkhorn 的差距。MESH 和 MESH-B 在多 seed 下给出更好的 memory-quality trade-off；论文强调 unblocked hidden-momentum 和 block-preconditioned variant 的总体表现接近，因此不要把收益简单归因于 block preconditioning。","reading_guide":"建议精读 Introduction、Background and Diagnosis、MESH 以及实验消融部分。Introduction 里最重要的是问题表述：expert 矩阵不能按 dense 二维矩阵处理。Background and Diagnosis 值得细看公式，尤其是 routed expert 梯度来自条件 token 分布，以及 Sinkhorn 非线性带来的 Sinkhorn(E[G]) 与 E[Sinkhorn(G)] 差异。MESH 部分重点看 hidden momentum 怎样接入 gradient buffer，而不是变成普通 AdamW 式显式状态。实验部分优先看三类结果：一是 AdamW、SAGE/Sinkhorn、MESH 的 loss-memory trade-off；二是 expert 与 non-expert 的分组 ablation；三是 scale、confidence、fused tensor、second moment 等负结果。真正值得深挖的是 optimizer state 统计口径和 hidden momentum 的实现边界：如果隐藏状态实际占用了与梯度生命周期相关的显存或通信带宽，它在大规模训练里的收益需要结合 ZeRO/FSDP、expert parallelism 和 activation/gradient overlap 重新核算。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper studies why memory-efficient Sinkhorn-style optimizers fail when moved from dense Transformer pretraining to MoE pretraining. A routed expert weight is a matrix, but its gradient is not sampled like a dense MLP gradient. Each expert sees a router-selected, gate-weighted token subset whose distribution changes during training. Applying stateless Sinkhorn normalization to this instantaneous expert gradient normalizes routing noise before any temporal smoothing. The central problem is therefore the ordering of operations: MoE experts need temporal averaging before matrix normalization, not per-step normalization of noisy conditional gradients.","importance":"The result matters for pretraining because optimizer state is a major memory cost in large MoE models. AdamW keeps two state tensors per parameter, which is especially expensive when the model has many expert parameters. The paper shows that optimizer assignment cannot be based only on tensor shape. Expert matrices and dense matrices have different gradient statistics because of routing. This is a useful lesson for MoE training recipes: optimizer design has to account for conditional token distributions and router-induced temporal variation. The contribution is mainly a controlled systems-and-optimization diagnosis rather than a full replacement for AdamW, but the failure mode is directly relevant to practical MoE pretraining.","technical_approach":"$c","experiments":"The controlled setting is a 110M-parameter nanowhale DeepSeek-style MoE pretraining run. A direct SAGE/Sinkhorn hybrid reduces optimizer state from 0.883GB to 0.331GB, but its evaluation loss reaches 3.8265, far worse than AdamW baselines in the same setup, which fall in the 3.58–3.64 range across studied seeds. The failure is localized to routed expert matrices. Applying SAGE to experts is worse. Reducing the Sinkhorn scale, fusing expert tensors before Sinkhorn normalization, or damping updates by routing confidence does not close the gap. Additional negative ablations cover second-moment-only, sign/momentum-only, confidence-only, scale-only, and fused-Sinkhorn variants. These results support the claim that the missing first-moment signal is the dominant issue. MESH and MESH-B improve the memory-quality trade-off across seeds, with the unblocked and block-preconditioned hidden-momentum variants reported as broadly similar in their multi-seed behavior.","reading_guide":"Read the Introduction and diagnosis sections carefully before looking at the optimizer details. The key idea is the mismatch between dense matrix gradients and routed expert gradients. The most important equations are the expert-gradient sampling expression and the argument that Sinkhorn(E[G]) differs from E[Sinkhorn(G)]. In the MESH section, focus on how hidden momentum is tied to the gradient-buffer lifecycle rather than added as ordinary optimizer state. In the experiments, prioritize the loss-memory comparison between AdamW, SAGE/Sinkhorn, and MESH; the expert versus non-expert ablations; and the negative results for scale tuning, confidence damping, fused Sinkhorn, and second-moment-only variants. A useful follow-up question is how the hidden state interacts with expert parallelism, gradient sharding, ZeRO/FSDP-style optimizer partitioning, and communication overlap in larger MoE runs.","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"DeepSeek实测的MoE训练内存优化方案，明确了无状态优化器在路由专家上失效的根因，应立刻在内部MoE训练框架中复现验证","en":"A DeepSeek-tested MoE training memory optimization that identifies the root cause of stateless optimizer failure on routed experts; reproduce immediately in internal MoE training stacks."},"metadata":{"institutions":["DeepSeek"],"authors":["Masato Fujitake"],"notable_authors":[],"tags":["MoE","optimizer","memory efficiency","LLM pretraining","Sinkhorn"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04407","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.04407","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"blog-hunyuan-(tencent)-89a23351","title":"From LR to ELR: A Better Heuristic for Pretraining Dynamics","abstract":"*!12 Figure 1. We bridge this gap by proposing MALT (Muon Augmented by Lightweight Two-sided Preconditioning), which uses lightweight diagonal preconditioners to reduce","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-08-05T17:26:47Z","category_id":"llm_pretrain","cover_url":"/covers/2608_05088.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"现有用于LLM预训练的矩阵优化器Muon仅通过Newton-Schulz迭代正交化动量来缓解梯度各向异性，但未显式建模损失景观的曲率各向异性（即Hessian矩阵条件数过大、不同参数方向的损失曲率差异悬殊的问题），在病态损失景观下收敛速度会显著下降。","importance":"该工作补充了Muon类矩阵优化器的曲率感知设计维度，提出的轻量对角预条件方案几乎不增加显存和训练耗时，为大模型预训练优化器的迭代提供了低开销的改进路径；“预条件正交化+噪声自适应步长”的组合思路，也为后续优化器设计提供了可复用的范式。","technical_approach":"提出MALT（带轻量双边预条件的Muon）及自适应变体MALTER：1）基于损失的局部二次近似，推导预条件空间下的Muon公式，用正定曲率代理构造变换空间使二次项各向同性，先对预条件后的动量正交化再映射回原参数空间，同时解决曲率与梯度各向异性问题；2）设计轻量双边对角预条件器：维护行/列平方梯度范数的指数移动平均（EMA），分别构造左、右对角预条件矩阵，保留参数的矩阵结构且额外开销极低；3）用范数嫁接（norm grafting）稳定更新尺度；4）MALTER额外在预条件空间中引入基于Adam式范数噪声自适应的标量步长，提升对随机梯度噪声的鲁棒性；同时给出了随机非凸优化场景下的收敛性保证。","experiments":"在GPT-2 Small、Medium、Large三个尺寸的语言模型预训练任务上评估：MALT和MALTER的显存占用、单步训练耗时与Muon基本持平，但性能一致优于Muon；其中MALTER整体表现最优，其相对Muon的性能提升幅度，与Muon相对AdamW的提升幅度相当，验证了预条件正交化与噪声自适应步长是互补的优化机制。","reading_guide":"泛读建议：重点看引言中曲率各向异性对Muon影响的玩具实验分析（图2）、MALT的预条件Muon公式推导部分（第3节）以及GPT-2预训练的对比实验结果（第5节）；收敛性证明可跳过，预计阅读时间20-25分钟。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"Muon, a matrix optimizer for LLM pretraining, only mitigates gradient anisotropy via Newton-Schulz momentum orthogonalization, but does not explicitly model curvature anisotropy of the loss landscape (i.e., excessively large Hessian condition number with sharp curvature discrepancies across parameter directions), leading to significantly slower convergence under ill-conditioned loss landscapes.","importance":"It supplements the curvature-aware design dimension for Muon-style matrix optimizers. 2.0","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-08-05T10:44:26Z","category_id":"architecture","cover_url":"/covers/2608_04678.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL","cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作问的是一个很直接但也很少被认真回答的问题：在极小参数规模、字节级输入、没有 tokenizer、没有 attention 的条件下，模型能不能不仅做分类，还能做自回归生成，而且在数据效率上真正有竞争力。作者把问题拆成三部分：一是 attention-free 架构在 next-byte language modeling 上是否随数据规模稳定优于同规模 transformer；二是如果不依赖更大 LLM 做 judge，怎样离线评估“小模型生成的文本像不像文本”；三是在小模型生成质量改善时，收益到底来自架构本身，还是来自 decoding policy 和 decode-time memory/retrieval。","importance":"对基座预训练研究，这篇文章的价值不在于它已经给出可替代 Transformer 的大规模路线，而在于它把几个常被混在一起的问题拆开了。第一，它提供了一个极小规模、严格受控的 setting，去检验 attention-free 架构的 data efficiency，而不是只看单点 perplexity。第二，它坚持 byte-level、tokenizer-free，这对 tokenizer 是否必要、离散表示是否限制小模型学习效率，提供了一个有意思的反例。第三，它把生成质量归因做得比较诚实：在这个尺度上，文本“形式感”更多来自 decoding 和检索短语，而不是 trunk 上再加一点结构。这一点对很多小模型论文是有提醒作用的。对大模型预训练的直接外推要很谨慎，但作为架构探索和实验方法论，它有启发。","technical_approach":"模型主干延续 Kathleen 系列前作。输入端是 ByteRotate encoder：不是为 256 个字节各学一个 embedding，而是从一个可学习向量出发，通过 DFT 相位旋转生成整个字节表征，因此整个 byte alphabet 只用 128 个参数覆盖。时序建模端是 multi-scale reverberant state，用三组不同时间常数的内容门控指数衰减状态来替代 attention。递推形式是 s_t = γ_t s_{t-1} + (1-γ_t) v_t，其中 γ_t 由内容决定，三组衰减范围分别覆盖短、中、长记忆。每个 reverb block 是 LayerNorm → reverb ∥ causal depthwise conv → gate mixing → FFN，堆三层，隐藏维 128。工程上新增 fast scan，把递推按 chunk 16 用 cumprod/cumsum 的闭式并行化，和逐步循环数值等价到 1e-7，带来 10–50× wall-clock 加速，这使得在免费 T4 上跑到 512MB 数据成为可能。对照组是参数匹配的 attention baseline：同样骨架，只把 reverb+conv 换成 4-head causal SDPA。除了 byte-level LM，作者还做了一个 3.08M 参数、10K 词表的 word-level 版本，用于生成研究。另一个技术点是评测工具 FORM DISTANCE。它是非参数、冻结、离线的“文本形式相似度”度量，目标不是测语义，而是测 sampled text 是否呈现出像自然文本的统计形式。作者还记录了该指标曾被某种策略“钻空子”，并给出补丁，这比直接用 LLM-as-a-judge 更可复现，也更适合小模型离线研究。最后，decode-time attribution study 系统比较了不同解码策略、检索短语记忆和若干 trunk 结构改动，试图回答生成改善到底来自哪里。","experiments":"最关键的是五点数据缩放实验。数据集是 WikiText-103 原始 UTF-8 字节流，训练数据规模取 2、8、32、128、512MB，固定 1MB 测试切片，训练 2 epochs，序列长度 256 bytes，AdamW + cosine，两个模型使用完全相同 recipe。结果上，reverb 模型在所有数据点都优于参数匹配 transformer。512MB 时，attention-free 模型 1.84 bits/byte，对照 transformer 2.04 bits/byte；32MB 时 reverb 为 2.011 bpb，而 transformer 即便训练到 512MB 也只有 2.043 bpb，作者据此给出“至少 16× 数据优势”的表述。log-log 斜率方面，REVERB 为 -0.074，ATTN 为 -0.107。作者提醒不要把更陡斜率误读成 attention 更优，因为那更像是从落后位置追赶；两条曲线都在变平。模型规模上，byte-level reverb 565,888 参数，attention baseline 463,360 参数。论文还提到在第二个数据集上做了复现，并用 frozen-probe transfer corroborate 主结论。生成部分的主结论更有意思：在这个小尺度上，文本形式质量主要由 decoding policy 和 retrieved phrases 决定，而不是 trunk 上额外的 architectural additions；作者报告了四个预注册的结构增强都没有带来帮助。这个负结果是可信号。需要注意的是，这里的优势主要体现在 byte-level、小模型、受限算力 setting；还不能推出在中大规模预训练上 attention-free 一定占优。","reading_guide":"泛读时建议按问题链来读。先看 architecture 一节，确认 ByteRotate 和 multi-scale reverb 到底替代了什么、代价是什么。再看 scaling 实验，重点盯住数据规模、参数匹配是否公平、训练 recipe 是否完全一致，以及 bpb 曲线而不是单点结果。之后看 FORM DISTANCE 的定义、验证和被 gaming 后的修补，这部分虽然不像主干架构那样显眼，但体现了作者对生成评测的克制态度。最后看 decode-time attribution study，尤其是哪些结构改动无效、哪些 decoding/retrieval 设定有效。预计 30 分钟左右可以抓住主结论。如果要深一点读，Appendix A 的 fast scan 值得看，它关系到这种递推状态模型能否在更大训练规模上具备工程可行性。阅读时要带着两个问题：一，这种 data efficiency 优势是否来自 byte-level 与 attention baseline 的某种不对称；二，FORM DISTANCE 测到的是“像文本的形式”，不是语义质量，不能把它当成通用生成能力指标。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper asks a clean question that is rarely tested under controlled conditions: can a tiny, byte-level, tokenizer-free, attention-free model do autoregressive generation competitively, not just classification, and can it do so with better data efficiency than a parameter-matched transformer? The authors break this into three subproblems: whether the architecture scales favorably on next-byte language modeling, how to evaluate “reads like text” without using a larger LLM as judge, and how to attribute generation gains between the trunk architecture, decoding policy, and decode-time memory or retrieval.","importance":"Its value for foundation-model research is not that it already offers a drop-in replacement for Transformer at large scale. The value is methodological. First, it studies data efficiency in a tightly controlled small-model regime instead of relying on isolated perplexity numbers. Second, it stays at raw-byte level with no tokenizer, which makes it relevant to questions about tokenization, discrete representations, and whether byte-level modeling is always too inefficient. Third, the attribution result is unusually honest: at this scale, text form is improved more by decoding and retrieved phrases than by adding more structure to the trunk. That is a useful corrective for many small-model architecture papers. The lessons should not be over-extrapolated to large-scale pretraining, but the paper is informative as an architecture probe and as an experimental design example.","technical_approach":"$e","experiments":"$f","reading_guide":"$10","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"不是能直接迁到大模型的证据，但它至少提醒两件事：attention-free 在小 regime 可能没你想的差，且生成观感常被 sampler 主导；只看 scaling 表和 FORM DISTANCE 即可。","en":"Not evidence you can directly port to frontier LLMs, but it usefully reminds us of two things: attention-free models may be less weak than assumed in small regimes, and perceived generation quality is often dominated by the sampler; the scaling table and FORM DISTANCE sections are enough."},"metadata":{"institutions":["Frederick University","Frederick University, Nicosia, Cyprus"],"authors":["George Fountzoulas"],"notable_authors":[],"tags":["attention-free","byte-level","autoregressive","small-scale-scaling","decoding"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04678","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.04678","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2608.04084","title":"SpecDrop: Parameter-Free Category-Conditioned Routing for Modular Specialization","abstract":"Mixture-of-experts (MoE) networks pursue specialization through learned routers, gates, and load-balancing losses, yet at matched total-parameter budgets learned routers can underperform equal-weight No-Routing baselines. Is the bottleneck the routing algorithm, or the alignment between training-signal granularity and the target categories? We probe the question with SpecDrop, a fixed parameter-free routing scheme: each of $K$ branches receives weight $p_a$ for its assigned category and a small ","starred":false,"priority":"p3","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-08-04T18:00:01Z","category_id":"architecture","cover_url":"/covers/2608_04084.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.CL","cs.CV"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇文章研究的是模块化网络里一个常被混在一起的问题：专家是否能学出真正的 specialization，究竟取决于路由器本身，还是取决于训练信号与划分类别之间是否对齐。作者的结论很明确：在很多场景里，瓶颈不在 learned router，而在 partition granularity 和 category clarity。也就是说，如果每个训练样本天然对应一个清晰类别，哪怕不用可学习路由，只用固定的按类 dropout，也能诱导出模块分工；如果样本本身跨域、跨技能、标签模糊，换更复杂的 router 也未必有用。","importance":"对 LLM 预训练的直接启发有限，但对 MoE/模块化 specialization 的研究有一个值得记住的负面结果：不要把专家不专门化简单归咎于 router 学得不好。对于语言建模，token、sequence、document 往往天然是 fuzzy partition，一个样本同时含多主题、多技能、多风格，因此 vision 里那种按单标签类别路由的收益未必能迁移到 LM。这个结论对做 domain-aware MoE、data-aware routing、instruction-specialized adapter 的人有参考价值：如果监督标签与训练单元不对齐，增加路由复杂度可能只是增加系统负担，而不会带来稳定 specialization。它更像一篇机制澄清论文，而不是能直接搬进大规模 pretrain recipe 的方法论文。","technical_approach":"SpecDrop 的设计很简单，也正因为简单，实验归因更干净。模型由 shared stem、K 个并行模块和 shared head 组成。作者先定义类别到模块的固定 assignment matrix，最简单是 round-robin，把 M 个类别均匀分给 K 个模块。训练时，对属于类别 c 的样本，其被分配模块以较高 keep probability p_a 激活，其他模块以较小 leakage probability p_i 激活，不是硬路由，因此仍保留少量跨类共享梯度。多个分支输出通过一个与类别无关的固定分母 S 做归一化合并，作者给出这个 fixed denominator 的性质：它让训练和测试的前向期望一致，并把 merged branch 的量级校准到单分支尺度，避免因为 dropout 采样导致幅度漂移。还可以加一个 always-on shared expert，承担通用表示。整个方法没有 learned routing parameter，也没有 load-balancing loss。作者的核心论证不是“固定路由优于 learned routing”，而是用一个几乎最小化设计的 routing scheme 去隔离变量：如果连这种无参数方案在清晰类别划分上都能学出 specialization，而在模糊划分上不能，那么问题更可能出在 partition alignment，而不是 router sophistication。文中还设计了 information-matched masking control：给 dense model 同样的类别标签信息，只在输出侧做掩码控制，用来区分“标签本身带来的性能增益”和“通过路由诱导模块结构带来的额外作用”。这个对照做得比较到位。","experiments":"实验覆盖四个设置，横跨 vision 和 language。结果最强的是对齐良好的视觉分类：在 CIFAR-100 和 ImageNet-1K 的 superclass 划分上，SpecDrop 明显优于不使用标签的参数匹配基线。摘要给出的数字是：CIFAR-100 上相对 dense 提升 +4.75 个 top-1 点；ImageNet-1K 上相对 matched No-Routing+SE 提升 +6.53 点，并且在作者评测的多分支 routing baseline 中最好。与此同时，作者用 information-matched masking control 说明，如果目标只是分类精度，直接把标签信息用于 dense 输出掩码往往更强；SpecDrop 的价值不在“更会用标签”，而在“把标签转成训练出来的模块结构”。到了 fuzzy partition 的 NLP 设定，结论反过来：在 SlimPajama-6B 语言建模和基于 Llama-3.2-1B 的 SuperNI/LoRA 指令微调上，SpecDrop 与匹配架构基线基本持平，落在 seed noise 范围内，没有稳定收益。这一点很重要，因为它直接限制了方法对 LLM 预训练的外推。换句话说，文章最有信息量的结果不是视觉上的正增益，而是语言上的“没有明显增益”，并且作者把原因归到训练单元的类别模糊性，而不是实现细节不足。","reading_guide":"泛读建议先看 Introduction 和 Figure 1，理解作者为什么把问题从“router 设计”转向“partition alignment”。然后看 Method 里的 assignment matrix、p_a/p_i dropout、fixed denominator S，这三处足够把方法吃透。实验部分重点看两类对照：一类是 aligned vision vs fuzzy NLP 的分裂结果，另一类是 information-matched masking control，它决定你如何解读性能来源。若你做 MoE 或 domain routing，预计 20 到 30 分钟能读完主线。若你做 LLM pretrain，只需要把语言实验和作者对 fuzzy partition 的解释看清楚，不必在视觉细节上停太久。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper asks a clean question about modular specialization: when experts fail to specialize, is the problem the routing algorithm, or the granularity and clarity of the partition used to supervise specialization? The main answer is that partition alignment matters more than router sophistication. If each training unit carries a clean category label, even a fixed parameter-free routing rule can induce specialization. If the training unit is intrinsically mixed or fuzzy, better routers do not buy much.","importance":"The direct impact on LLM pretraining is limited, but the paper is useful as a cautionary result for MoE and modular design. It argues against a common reflex: blaming weak specialization on the learned router alone. In language modeling, tokens, sequences, and documents are often fuzzy mixtures of topics, skills, and styles, so the clean single-category assumptions that help in vision may not transfer. For work on domain-aware MoE, data-aware routing, or instruction-specialized adapters, the practical lesson is that routing complexity cannot compensate for poorly aligned supervision. At a compression ratio of 0.30, Be","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-08-05T07:58:53Z","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2608_04569.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL","cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作指出硬提示压缩中的一个结构性失效：压缩器独立选择高相关 token、句子或段落时，可能保留答案片段，却删除解释该片段所需的前置实体定义或桥接证据。作者称这种现象为 referential dangling。","importance":"这项结果对长上下文推理和推理侧降本有直接启发。很多 prompt compression 方法优化的是局部相关性或重要性分数，但多跳问答需要的是完整证据链。保留“看起来相关”的片段并不等于保留“可推理”的上下文。对基座研究来说，它提醒长上下文能力评估不能只看召回率或压缩后 token 数，也要检查证据依赖是否闭合。对 serving 和压缩系统来说，它说明压缩目标需要从独立片段打分走向依赖感知选择。","technical_approach":"作者先形式化 extractive hard compressor：给定上下文、问题和 token 预算，压缩器输出源文本片段的子序列。referential dangling 被定义为：压缩结果保留了任务相关句子，但删除了使该句子可解释、可用于回答问题的支持片段。例如保留答案实体和一个地理事实，却删除了把问题主体连接到该地理事实的桥接句。实验先诊断 Beaver 这类基于 embedding 相似度和 chunk 排序的压缩器，再扩展到多种压缩方法。随后做两类修复：一类是 annotation-guided reselection，把遗漏的支持段落插回，并用删除最低分非支持段落抵消预算；另一类是自动 restoration，从被删除文本中生成候选句，训练小型分类器判断这些句子是否是解释已保留文本所需的依赖，再插回高分候选。","experiments":"在 compression ratio 0.30 下，Beaver 在 HotpotQA、2WikiMultiHopQA 和 MuSiQue 的 bridge examples 上造成 34% 到 54% 的答案路径不完整。人工审计显示 dangling 诊断 precision 为 95%。在共享 HotpotQA bridge 集合上，6 个压缩器都出现该问题，比例为 32% 到 60%。LongBench-v2 Single-Document QA 的 80 个文档全部包含至少一个 dangling reference。修复实验使用 Qwen3-8B 作为下游答题模型：在 dangling examples 上，插回遗漏支持段落并保持预算不变，准确率提升 29 到 34 个百分点，p < 1e-4，并恢复原压缩上下文与完整支持上下文之间至少 88% 的准确率差距。自动 restoration 在 HotpotQA 上将准确率提升 4.7 个百分点，压缩率仅从 0.30 变为 0.31。","reading_guide":"泛读建议 20 到 30 分钟。先看 Introduction 中的 Figure 1 和问题定义，理解为什么答案字符串存在仍然可能不可用。然后看 Problem Formulation 中 Sufficiency 的定义，它是这篇工作的核心抽象。实验部分重点看三组数字：Beaver 在三套多跳 QA 上的 dangling 率、6 个压缩器的横向结果、annotation-guided reselection 的准确率恢复幅度。自动 restoration 部分可以快速看方法框架和 HotpotQA 结果，主要价值在于证明依赖修复可以部分自动化。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper identifies a structural failure mode in hard prompt compression. Independent token, sentence, or chunk selection can keep a relevant fragment while removing the antecedent, entity definition, or bridge fact needed to interpret it. The authors call this failure referential dangling.","importance":"The result matters for long-context inference and cost reduction. Many compressors optimize local relevance, perplexity, embedding similarity, attention-derived importance, or learned salience. Multi-hop reasoning instead requires a closed evidence path. A compressed prompt can contain the answer string and still be unusable because the dependency that links the query to that answer has been removed. This is a useful diagnostic for pretraining and inference research around long context: recall of relevant spans is not enough; dependency completeness should be measured.","technical_approach":"The work formalizes extractive hard compression as selecting a source-ordered subset of the input under a token budget. A dangling case occurs when a task-relevant retained sentence lacks sufficient retained support to be interpreted and used in the evidence chain for the query. The empirical study starts with Beaver, a chunk-ranking compressor using Qwen3-0.6B embeddings, and then checks whether the issue generalizes to other compressors. Two repair strategies are studied. The first is annotation-guided reselection: reinsert the omitted supporting paragraph and remove the lowest-scoring non-supporting paragraphs to keep the token budget fixed. The second is automatic restoration: generate candidate sentences from omitted text and train a compact classifier to rank whether a candidate is needed to interpret retained content, then reinsert top-ranked candidates without using support annotations at inference time.","experiments":"At a 0.30 compression ratio, Beaver leaves answer paths incomplete in 34% to 54% of bridge examples across HotpotQA, 2WikiMultiHopQA, and MuSiQue. A human audit reports 95% precision for the dangling diagnosis. The issue is not specific to Beaver: on a shared HotpotQA bridge set, all six tested compressors show dangling rates between 32% and 60%. In LongBench-v2 Single-Document QA, all 80 documents contain at least one dangling reference. With Qwen3-8B as the downstream answer model, annotation-guided reselection improves accuracy by 29 to 34 points on dangling examples at the same token budget, with p < 1e-4, and recovers at least 88% of the gap between the original compressed context and a context retaining both annotated supporting paragraphs. The automatic restoration method improves HotpotQA accuracy by 4.7 points while changing the compression ratio only from 0.30 to 0.31.","reading_guide":"Skim in about 20 to 30 minutes. Start with the motivating example and the formal definition of sufficiency, since that is the main conceptual contribution. Then read the diagnostic results for Beaver and the six-compressor comparison. The most informative ablation is the fixed-budget reselection experiment, because it separates missing dependency structure from mere token-budget shortage. The automatic restoration section is useful as a first step toward dependency-aware compression, but the larger lesson is the evaluation failure of independent relevance scoring.","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"这篇值得看，因为它把“压缩掉信息”细化成“压缩断了依赖对”；如果你在做 long-context pruning，只看 dangling 定义、干预实验和错误率表就够，足以改内部设计。","en":"Worth reading because it sharpens “compression loses information” into “compression breaks dependency pairs”; if you work on long-context pruning, the dangling definition plus the intervention tables are enough to change your design instincts."},"metadata":{"institutions":["Qinghai University","Tsinghua University","Ant Group Security and Intelligence Laboratory","Ant Group Security and Intelligence Laboratory (SIL)"],"authors":["Zhengpei Hu","Kai Li","Dapeng Fu","Xuechao Zou","Yuanhao Tang","Yue Li","Tengfei Cao","Jianqiang Huang"],"notable_authors":[],"tags":["long-context","prompt-compression","failure-analysis","multi-hop-qa","retrieval-dependency"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04569","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.04569","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2608.04962","title":"SpecRoll: Fast-Slow Verifier-Feedback Adaptation for Speculative Reinforcement Learning Rollouts","abstract":"Reinforcement learning (RL) post-training improves the reasoning capabilities of large language models, but autoregressive rollout generation remains a major efficiency bottleneck. di","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-08-05T15:32:27Z","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2608_04962.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作处理的是 dominates wall-clock time. Speculative decoding is a natural acceleration tool, but RL makes it harder because the target policy keeps changing. A static proposer quickly becomes stale, while continuously training a separate drafter adds backward passes, optimizer state, synchronization overhead, and extra KV-cache cost. The core problem is how to make speculative rollouts adapt to a non-stationary policy while preserving the exact target sampling distribution and keeping the system lightweight.","importance":"This is not a pretraining paper in the narrow sense, but it matters for the pretrain-to-post-train interface and for large-scale training systems. Second, it introduces a two-timescale adaptation scheme: local, gradient-free, within-trajectory correction for transient mismatch, and slower parameter updates for persistent drift. That separation is relevant beyond RL, including continual pretraining and other non-stationary training loops. In acquisition, positions are ranked by a composite score multiplying three factors: concentration of teacher probability mass in a small top-k candidate set, teacher entropy at the position, and distribution/ranking mismatch between student and teacher over the candidate set. In exploration, for selected positions, each candidate in the teacher's top-k is appended to the current prefix, rolled out under the student policy, and scored by a task verifier.
